- **S2.** Defect (11)'s test asserts that `run(Stage::Fr9)` publishes AL-F3 under `AlM5Omitted`.
- **S3 to S5.** Wording: the fixtures run only in the debug AL-I3 test; `LadderFault` and the `fault()` call sites compile into non-test builds as constant `false`; the CI-layer AL-I3 sample and the ignored corpus run are stated above, with the AL-U3 note on `collect_boundary`.

### Alt-A1 AL-U2 implementation

Unit AL-U2 of the [Alt-A1 production contract](p3-alt-a1-fill-ladder-contract.md#61-units): the permanent oracles and the identity tooling. The ladder is still not wired into the export, so no export output changes. Acceptance for this unit: AL-A2, AL-A7 (U2 part), AL-A8, AL-A12 and AL-A16. AL-U1 was merged through PR #154 (`f00f27d`). This entry records the implementation; it makes no performance claim, and precision stays UNVERIFIED.

Files:

- New `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/fill_ladder_dump_tests.rs` (`cfg(test)`): the ignored hand-off `emit_fill_ladder_dump`, a debug test of its input and framing, and `OnceScan`, a statement-for-statement copy of V2's `collect_boundary` for the harnesses.
- New `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/fill_ladder_identity.rs` (`cfg(test)`, branch-only): the rung record `dump_rungs` and a debug test of its line format.
- `lib.rs`: the two `cfg(test)` module declarations, appended at the end.
- `p3_b0r_identity.rs`: data cases, and no API call. `dump_native_digests` also reads `p3-fill-ladder-random/v1` (`P3_FILL_LADDER_CORPUS`, same frame) through its existing `read_random`. After the review (S2), its single `meta/workspace-sizes` case is split into `meta/cubic-workspace-sizes` (`size_of::<CubicFillWorkspace>()` and `allocated_bytes()`) and `meta/fill-engine-sizes` (`FillEngine`).
- New `tests/geometry/p3-fill-ladder/`: `corpus.ts` (the family, the `P3FLDIN1` dump input, the fixture inputs), `dump.ts` (the strict dump reader) and `oracle.ts` (the section 4.2 extensions).
- New `tests/geometry/p3-fill-ladder-oracle.test.ts`, inside `pnpm test:geometry`.
- `tooling/compare-p3-fill-identity.mjs` and `.d.mts`: the allow-list, the revision check, the ladder data cases, `--revision-change` and `--self-test`.
- `tests/unit/p3-b0r-identity.test.ts`: the new mode and the allow-list, the only existing test file this unit changes (section 4.4's AL-U2 row). The spike oracle module did not need the backward-compatible change, so it is unchanged.

The spike module, its tests, its harness, its oracle module and its oracle test are unchanged, and so is `fill_ladder_tests.rs`.

Design:

- **`p3-fill-ladder-random/v1`** (section 4.2). One family, `L1-generic-ladder`, of 2,000 paths, xorshift32 (13, 17, 5) from seed `0x4c414452`, recorded in the generator. Each path has 1-4 rings with generic, unsnapped coordinates, side by side or nested, each reversed with probability 1/2. Edges are cubic, line or mixed, with B0's bulges, and both fill rules are drawn. The exponent is e = e₀ + p, with e₀ in {−4, −3, −2} for edges of length 4-16, and the coordinates are scaled exactly by 2^p, in three strata (widened after the review, S5): p in [−40, 40] for 70% of the paths; |e| in [400, 510], inside AL-FR0's range, for 15% (306 paths); and |p| in [515, 1000] for 15%, so that e leaves the range (321 paths, e from −999 to 993, inside V2's −1021 ≤ e ≤ 1023). |p| ≤ 1000 keeps every coordinate finite (the nonzero ones lie between about 3.5·10^-300 and 6.7·10^301). The family is written in the `p3-b0r-random/v1` frame (`P3BRRND1`) with SHA-256 `a597510d…7bbc`, pinned in the identity tool and checked by both test layers. The CI dump set takes its first 200 paths.
- **The hand-off** (section 4.2). `emit_fill_ladder_dump` reads a `P3FLDIN1` input of named entries and writes one framed JSON line per entry (schema `p3-fill-ladder-dump/1`, with the kernel's `fill_v0_revision()` in the header). A real path runs:
  - `FillLadder::run`, inside a tracking-allocator window: rung, reason, AL-FR9 item, certified bound, fast mesh and the edges used once;
  - the S1 prefix alone, for the S1 view;
  - `exact_cascade` alone, on its own workspace (the module-level form of AL-U3's `exact_only`): step, status, published polygon and mesh, and whether the ladder's own exact result equals it;
  - the frozen spike's rung 0 at the path's δ (AL-I3);
  - the export on the one-path batch: status, positions, indices and boundary pairs.

  A synthetic view runs rung 0 alone and the spike's. Fault names listed with an entry switch `LadderFault`s on around it (the seeded defects).

- **Revision awareness.** The new test files are existing test files from AL-U3 on, and AL-U3's allow-list does not include them. The oracles therefore read the revision from the dump. With revision 1 they judge the module's fast mesh and its scan. From revision 2 they judge the export's output and also require it to equal the module's fast mesh bit for bit.
- **Oracles** (`oracle.ts`), as extensions that import the spike verifiers unchanged:
  - δ = 2^e per entry, exactly;
  - `RANGE` is right exactly when e lies outside [−511, 510], and any other outcome outside it is an `AL-FR0` soundness failure;
  - `MESH_CHECK` passes FR1-FR8 and names no condition, and its count must be 0 outside seeded defects;
  - AL-M5 is checked on every degenerate-source close edge in the exact form b < D, D + b − a > 0 and 4·D·b < (D + b − a)². Here e′'s source piece is re-derived from the owners, a cubic leaf restricted exactly by blossoming;
  - SL-O2 adds a check that `boundaryEdges` equal the edges used once, pair for pair, in triangle order;
  - SL-O3 uses τ = 2^(e−1);
  - AL-I3 allows only `RANGE` outside the range and an AL-M5 `CLOSURE` where the spike passes FR3.

  A conservative fallback (an undecided interval) is allowed, as in the spike's oracle run. Only the fixture views must have exact reasons.

- **Identity tool** (section 4.4):
  - `--allow-test-change <path>` repeats. A listed file that is not changed fails, and so does a changed existing test file that is not listed (the source path of a rename counts). The list, the allowed `git diff --name-status` lines and the full listing are written to `allowed-test-changes.json` in the run directory.
  - `loadFillKernel` accepts any revision ≥ 1. Plain mode requires equal revisions; `--revision-change` requires the branch to be the merge base's plus one. The run stores both in `revisions.json`, and `validateChangedPaths` refuses a dump whose header revision is not the branch kernel's.
  - `--revision-change` runs `fill_ladder_identity::dump_rungs` on the branch and classifies every native cascade case and WASM case as unchanged, fast-identical, changed-fast or newly-admitted. It fails a rung-1 case whose bytes differ, a case missing from the rung record, a rung-0 case whose branch status is not `ok`, and any changed native entry or meta case (AL-I1), except `meta/fill-engine-sizes`, exempt by exact name and recorded. It runs `emit_fill_ladder_dump` on the paths of every changed case and requires each to be fast with no SL-O1 or SL-O2 failure. It writes `revision-change-manifest.tsv` (case, class, old status, reason), its SHA-256 and the counts per layer and family, and its self-check tampers a rung-1 case, which must fail by name.
  - `--compare-only <dir> --revision-change` re-checks a finished run, including the revision rule from its `revisions.json` (a missing file fails in that mode).
  - `--self-test` builds a synthetic run directory with one case of each class. It requires that revision-change mode passes the clean directory and fails it after a seeded unexpected change or with a branch revision that is not base + 1, and that plain mode refuses the changed cases.
  - The ladder family joins the native layer (10,000 more cases) and the WASM layer (2,000 more cases). The tool's version string is now `compare-p3-fill-identity/2`.

Interpretations recorded for review:

- **Where the hand-off lives.** Section 6.1 lists no Rust file for `emit_fill_ladder_dump`. It is in a new `fill_ladder_dump_tests.rs` rather than in `fill_ladder_tests.rs`, which AL-U2 may not change, or in the branch-only rung-record file.
- **`OnceScan`.** `fill_ladder_tests.rs`'s `TestScan` is private to that file, so the hand-off and the rung record use a second statement-for-statement copy of `collect_boundary`. AL-U3 replaces neither: the rung record keeps computing the rung through the module (section 4.4), and the hand-off's export fields carry the real `collect_boundary` from revision 2 on.
- **Fixture views through the spike's hand-off.** SL-F1 to SL-F21 are passed to the Rust dump as the S1 views of the spike's unchanged `emit_spike_fixtures`, so the production rung runs on exactly the views the spike's oracles validated, without a third copy of the fixture builders. AL-F3 and AL-F4 are rebuilt in TypeScript as `al_closure_rectangle` builds them. The real-path fixtures (AL-F1, AL-F2, AL-F5, AL-F6 and AL-F7's synthetic inputs) are rebuilt as `fill_ladder_tests.rs` builds them. AL-F7's corpus inputs and AL-F9 are B0 paths in the dump set.
- **Seeded defects in the oracle test.** Five defect entries check that the TypeScript oracles reject what the Rust tests reject. Defect (11) is flagged by AL-M5. Defect (12) is flagged by AL-FR0. Defect (13) with AL-F8b (a) and (c) is flagged by SL-O2 item 3. AL-F8 falls back with `MESH_CHECK` from item 1.
- **`meta/` cases in revision-change mode.** AL-U3 changes `FillEngine`'s sizes when it reserves the ladder, so `--revision-change` exempts `meta/fill-engine-sizes`, by exact name, and records it in the summary. `meta/cubic-workspace-sizes` and every native entry case must stay identical (AL-I1).

Independent diff review (`reviewer`): READY, no blocking items. Its should-fix items are applied on the same branch:

- **S1, the revision-2 branch.** A new test, `judges a revision-2 export output and decodes the revision-1 export`, does three things. It copies a fast path's module mesh and boundary pairs into its export fields and requires `evaluateEntry(…, 2)` to report no failure. It rotates one triangle and reverses one pair, and requires the SL-O2 failure "the export output differs from the module fast mesh" for each. At revision 1 it requires `verifyBoundaryEdges` to be empty on the decoded export block of every `ok` path, 262 in the CI dump set, so the decoder runs on V2's own output.

  **Faults on the export path.** `emit_fill_ladder_dump` switches an entry's faults on with `with_ladder_faults` around the whole entry, export call included. That call runs on the same thread, and `fill_ladder`'s `fault()` reads the thread-local mask. So once AL-U3's `build_path` calls rung 0, defects (13a) and (13c) reach the export too: it publishes the defective mesh, which SL-O2 rejects at revision 2. AL-U3 needs no edit of these files for that. It has to keep rung 0 behind `fill_ladder`'s `fault()` call sites and run the export on the calling thread, both of which hold today.

- **S2, the meta case.** It is split as the Files list says. `--revision-change` exempts only `meta/fill-engine-sizes`, by exact name. A changed `meta/cubic-workspace-sizes` fails as AL-I1, and a unit test covers it.
- **S3, the revision in re-checks.** The full run writes `revisions.json`. `--compare-only` applies the revision rule from it in both modes; in revision-change mode a missing file fails. `validateChangedPaths` takes the branch kernel's revision and refuses a dump with another one. The self-test adds a branch revision that is not base + 1, which must fail.
- **S4, end-to-end smoke test.** A new geometry test, `validates changed paths end to end`, runs the tool's own `pathSources` → `encodeDumpInput` → cargo `emit_fill_ladder_dump` → `evaluateEntry` on three real paths (`b0/P2/s=3/0000`, `b0/O1/P2/s=3/0000`, `rnd/L1-generic-ladder/0001`). Each rung must match the CI dump, `applyOracleValidation` must pass a changed-fast case and fail a newly admitted case whose path fell back, and a dump from another revision must be refused.
- **S5, exponent coverage.** The family's strata are widened as the Design section says, and the SHA-256 is re-pinned to `a597510d…7bbc`. The family's `v1` identity is first pinned in this unit, so no earlier record depends on the old bytes.
- **Minor.** The `cargo test` counts are reworded, and the `--compare-only` line now states the revision re-check.
- **Known limitations M1-M5** (from the independent review, recorded by the Primary):
  - M1: the AL-I3 `CLOSURE` allowance (`oracle.ts`, and the same rule in `fill_ladder_tests.rs`) cannot tell an AL-M5 `CLOSURE` from an SL-M1 to SL-M4 port divergence. It matches the contract literally; once AL-U3 is in, the plain identity run catches such a regression.
  - M2: the self-test's seeded leg is confounded, because tampering the native cascade also breaks the WASM-against-native check. The classifier is still proven by the internal `revisionSelfCheck` in the clean leg and by the unit tests.
  - M3: `applyOracleValidation` gates only SL-O1 and SL-O2, the contract minimum. Harness and AL-I3 results are recorded, not gated.
  - M4: `STRUCTURE` from FR6 is not in `AFTER_FR3` (`oracle.ts`). This is harmless, because it is a fallback.
  - M5: O5 does not protect `fill_ladder_identity.rs`. This is by design and fails safe; the AL-A9 reviewer should know that only the manifest counts depend on it.

### Alt-A1 AL-U3 implementation

Unit AL-U3 of the [Alt-A1 production contract](p3-alt-a1-fill-ladder-contract.md#61-units), the switch: the ladder runs in the fill export, under the new operation identity. Acceptance for this unit: AL-A2, AL-A4 (U3 part), AL-A5, AL-A6 (U3 part), AL-A7, AL-A8, AL-A9, AL-A10, AL-A12 and AL-A16. AL-U2 was merged through PR #155 (`a4b20f3`). This entry records the implementation; it makes no performance claim, and precision stays UNVERIFIED.

Files:

- `fill_export.rs`: step 0 in `build_path`, revision 2, rung 0's storage, `BoundaryScan` for `FillScratch`, `exact_only` and the seeded defects (14) and (15).
- `packages/geometry-wasm/src/fill-adapter.ts`: `FILL_V0_OPERATION_ID` is `'geometry-wasm/fill-v0+r2'`, `FILL_V0_REVISION` is 2, and the `operationId` literal type of `FillMeshAdapter` follows.
- New test file `fill_export_ladder_tests.rs`, with its `cfg(test)` declaration appended at the end of `lib.rs`, as AL-U1 and AL-U2 appended theirs.
- Existing test files, each edit listed under "Edits of existing tests" below: `fill_export_tests.rs` and `tests/geometry/fill-adapter.test.ts`.
- The section 1.4 documents: the [graphics architecture](../graphics-engine-architecture.md#primitive-rendering) gains the section 1.5 paragraph, dated 2026-10-20. The [private contract](p3-private-contract.md#fill-topology-and-independent-verification) gains its pointer paragraph. The [V2 contract](p3-v2-fill-mesh-transport-contract.md) gains six dated pointer lines: at the identity table; at section c, twice; at section f; at section g; and at section h. The [V3 contract](p3-v3-renderer-mesh-pipeline-contract.md) gains three: at the source facts, the fringe builder and the V2 dependencies. No frozen or approved text changes.
- Declared deviation, by the Primary's decision: `tooling/compare-p3-fill-identity.mjs` and `.d.mts`, AL-U2's identity tool, which O5 does not protect. Its full-run tamper self-check is fixed for revision-change mode (see "The re-baseline" below).
- In-scope cleanups, comment-only: `fill_ladder.rs` (the module header and the `BoundaryScan` doc) and `lib.rs` (the `fill_ladder` declaration comment) no longer say that the ladder is not yet wired into the export. Line counts are unchanged, and the release WASM SHA-256 is the same.

The spike module and the AL-U2 oracles, dump and identity-record files are unchanged; `fill_ladder.rs` changes only in the comments above. The AL-U2 oracle test passes at revision 2 without edits: its dump header reads revision 2, and the oracles judge the export's output.

Design:

- **Step 0** (sections 1.2, 3.1 and 3.8). After V2's stages 1-3, `build_path` calls `FillLadder::fast_rung` on the path's request. If rung 0 publishes, the path's result is the fast mesh. No exact attempt, no cell-ladder refinement and no ledger cross-check run. On a fallback, V2's two-step cascade runs on the same input; its statements are unchanged, and only the test switch of defect (14) can invert its order. `build_path` returns a `BuiltPath` record: the outcome, the rung, and, on a fallback, the reason and the exact step (section 3.6). `run_batch` takes a fast mesh's vertices from the ladder and an exact mesh's from the workspace. Both take the triangles and boundary pairs from the fill scratch.
- **`BoundaryScan` for `FillScratch`: the copy.** `scan` clears the scratch, copies the fast triangles into its reserved index buffer and calls V2's `collect_boundary` unchanged. The index buffer holds 2,304 slots, and rung 0 needs at most 222. The copy is chosen over a new signature for `collect_boundary` because the copy changes no line of the exact path, so the identity-checked refinement, scan and ledger cross-check stay byte-identical. It also gives a fast mesh's boundary pairs the slot order that the AL-U2 oracles require. After a published fast mesh, the scratch holds exactly the published triangles and pairs.
- **Storage** (section 3.4). `build_workspace` reserves `FillLadder::reserve(FILL_V0_LIMITS)` after the scratch and the workspace. That reservation also checks the scratch's capacity for AL-FR9's records. A refused reservation is `ALLOCATION_FAILED`, as before. The reserve-time total adds `allocated_bytes()` (28,760 bytes), so `heap_bytes` and the `meta/fill-engine-sizes` case change. `linearMemoryBytes()` is the instance's memory size and includes the reservation. `dispose` and a failed reservation drop the ladder. `process` needs both the workspace and the ladder.
- **`exact_only`, defects (14) and (15).** `TestHooks` gains `exact_only`, `defect_cascade_inverted` and `defect_fallback_partial`. They reach `build_path` as a `PathSwitches` value, which outside test builds is an empty type whose switches are constant `false`.
  - Defect (14) inverts the cascade: triangle-free first, and Legacy after `KnotMismatch` or `Unresolved`.
  - Defect (15) skips the exact mesh's refinement on a fallback path. The path is then served from the scratch as rung 0, or an earlier fast path, left it.
- **Reasons through the export.** The test files fixed each expected reason before their first run, and every one held: C15's hanging fixtures, F04, F05, F07, F10 (B as given and reversed) and F11 under both rules, and the transverse bowtie, are `CROSSING`. The linear-cubic bowtie and the `KnotMismatch` control are `MONOTONE`. AL-F1 is `RANGE`. A command-limit cubic and a 25-verb line path are `PREFIX`. SL-F15 is `STRUCTURE`; `O1/P2/s=3#0` (embedded as bits) is `CLOSURE`; AL-F6 and F06 are `MONOTONE`; SL-F3 is `CLEARANCE`. No real input reaches `DEVIATION` or `TRIANGLE` (AL-U1's finding); the extended allocation test seeds `TRIANGLE` and `MESH_CHECK` with AL-U1's fault switches.

Seeded defects of this unit (AL-A4), in `fill_export_ladder_tests.rs`:

| Defect                            | Test                                                      | Observed failure                                                                                                                                                     |
| --------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| (14) cascade inverted             | `al_u3_defect_14_cascade_inverted_is_detected`            | The linear-cubic bowtie (`Unresolved` twice) ends with `transverse_topology_invoked` false, against its existing assertion; AL-F6's ladder record shows a retry step |
| (15) fallback from rung 0's state | `al_u3_defect_15_fallback_from_partial_state_is_detected` | Behind a fast F01, each fallback fixture with an exact mesh (at least 10) fails the batch or differs from `exact_only`                                               |

**Finding on defect (14)'s first detector.** The contract names AL-F6's `!transverse_topology_invoked` as the detector, marked "to verify before the first run". It was verified, and it does not fire. `attempt_triangle_free` returns `Unresolved` on AL-F6, so the inverted cascade retries Legacy, which admits the path with V2's mesh and resets the diagnostics. The output and the flag are therefore unchanged. The second detector, the linear-cubic bowtie, fires. The test also asserts AL-F6's native ladder record, whose exact step becomes `TriangleFree`.

Edits of existing tests (section 4.4's AL-U3 row; the identity run's allow-list):

- `fill_export_tests.rs`:
  - `FILL_V0_REVISION` 1 → 2 (`runtime_limits_equal_the_native_test_limits`).
  - One `exact_only` line, on the line-only engine of `cascade_retries_triangle_free_only_after_knot_or_unresolved`. Through the ladder the square at e = 0 is fast, so the assertion "line-only paths skip topology" would no longer exercise the exact rung. The sibling `al_u3_line_only_square_takes_rung_zero_and_no_topology` asserts the ladder's behavior on the same input. No other existing test needed `exact_only`: each still passes unchanged, and C15's fixtures and V2's controls fall back.
  - The extended `process_does_not_allocate_or_move_storage`. It adds six paths (ids 7-12): one new fast square, and `RANGE`, `PREFIX`, `STRUCTURE`, `CLOSURE` and `CLEARANCE` inputs; the fast square with id 1 already existed. It asserts each path's rung and reason, and runs two more processes under `TriangleFlipped` (`MESH_CHECK`) and `Fr7OrientationRejected` (`TRIANGLE`). Every window has zero allocations and the same storage, which now includes rung 0's.
- `tests/geometry/fill-adapter.test.ts`: the `operationId` literal becomes `'geometry-wasm/fill-v0+r2'`. One override line, `{ fill_v0_revision: () => 1 }`, adds the refused revision-1 kernel (AL-A10).

New tests, in `fill_export_ladder_tests.rs`, all debug and inside `pnpm test:geometry`:

- **Section 3.7 and AL-I2:** the fallback fixtures above. Each is checked through `build_path` (rung, reason, and equality of outcome and step with `exact_only`). In a one-path batch, the export bytes equal the `exact_only` bytes. In one multi-path batch behind a fast F01, each path's record (without `blockOffset`) and block bytes equal the `exact_only` ones.
- **AL-F8 and AL-F8b through the export:** `MESH_CHECK`, then the `exact_only` bytes.
- **Fast meshes:**
  - AL-F5: F01 under both rules. Positions are the square's vertex bits, indices are rung 0's triangles, the pairs are the four directed edges, and there are two triangles with no refinement.
  - AL-F2 at e = 510 (`CLEARANCE`, equal to `exact_only`) and e = −511 (fast, area 100).
  - AL-F9: both B0 fixture paths are fast through the export and differ from their exact-rung meshes.
- **Storage:** `heap_bytes` = workspace + scratch + `size_of::<FastRung>()`, and dispose drops rung 0.

- Oracle gate: `emit_fill_ladder_dump` ran on the 7,635 paths of every changed case. All 7,635 are fast, with 0 SL-O1 or SL-O2 failures (`oracle-validation.json`, dump SHA-256 `41ed88c3…ec12`). No rung-1 case changed bytes, no case is missing from the rung record, and the internal revision-change self-check detected its tampered rung-1 case (`b0/O1/P2/s=3/0000/fill_v0_cascade`).
- AL-I1: the 61,080 native entry cases are identical. Only the cascade cases and the exempt `meta/fill-engine-sizes` differ, and every WASM case equals its native cascade digest.
- O5: the modified kernel sources are `fill_export.rs`, `fill_export_tests.rs`, `fill_ladder.rs` (comments) and `lib.rs`. The changed test files are exactly the two allowed ones (`allowed-test-changes.json`).
- Tamper self-checks: `b0/P2/s=3/0000/attempt` (an AL-I1 entry case) and `wasm/rnd/F1-generic-rings/0007` (an unchanged WASM case), both detected. The run's failure list is empty.
- `node tooling/compare-p3-fill-identity.mjs --compare-only artifacts/p3-b0r-identity/20261019T182542Z-646e543c --revision-change`: PASS (exit 0), with the same manifest SHA-256 and counts.

**Identity-tool fix, a declared deviation.** The first run, `20261019T171901Z-3882b881` (60 min 35 s), had an empty classification failure list, passed the oracle gate, held AL-I1, and produced the same manifest. It still exited FAIL, on the full run's plain tamper self-checks (`rnd/F1-generic-rings/0000/attempt` and `wasm/b0/P2/s=3/0000`, "not detected"). `tamperSelfCheck` requires that a tampered branch map differ from the base in exactly one case. In revision-change mode, thousands of cascade and WASM cases already differ by design, so that cannot hold. This is an AL-U2 defect: AL-U2's `--self-test` never reached the full run's tamper step.

By the Primary's decision, AL-U3 fixes it in `tooling/compare-p3-fill-identity.mjs`:

- A new exported `revisionTamperChecks` tampers, each on its own, the first native AL-I1 entry case and the first WASM case of the `unchanged` class. The classification must then report each by name, as an AL-I1 entry difference or as a rung-1 case whose bytes differ.
- The full run uses it in revision-change mode. Plain mode keeps `tamperSelfCheck` unchanged, and the existing rung-1 `revisionSelfCheck` stays.
- `--self-test` gains a leg for it on the synthetic directory. `rnd/T/0000/attempt` and `wasm/rnd/T/0000` must both be detected.
- `.d.mts` declares the function. `tests/unit/p3-b0r-identity.test.ts` is not on AL-U3's allow-list and is unchanged.
- `node tooling/compare-p3-fill-identity.mjs --self-test`: PASS. Clean revision-change passes; the seeded unexpected change, both revision-change tamper checks, a revision that is not base + 1, and plain mode on changed cases are each detected.

### Alt-A1 AL-U4a diagnostic split

Unit AL-U4a of the [Alt-A1 production contract](p3-alt-a1-fill-ladder-contract.md#61-units): a `cfg(test)` sub-stage split of the certificate FR1-FR5, functional only ([section 5.1](p3-alt-a1-fill-ladder-contract.md#51-a-certificate-stage-optimization), process item AL-U4a). It locates where the certificate's time goes, to guide the outcome-identical optimizations AL-C1 to AL-C4 of AL-U4b. It implements no optimization and changes no output. Acceptance for this unit: AL-A2, AL-A12 and AL-A16, and AL-A8 by the argument below. AL-U3 was merged through PR #156 (`f7d711b`). The timings are diagnostics, **not evidence**: this entry makes no performance claim, and precision stays UNVERIFIED.

Files:

- New test file `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/fill_ladder_diag_tests.rs`, with its `cfg(test)` declaration appended at the end of `lib.rs`, as AL-U1 to AL-U3 appended theirs.
- No other source changes. `fill_ladder.rs` and every non-test source are unchanged, so the release WASM is byte-identical to `main`'s, and the native entry and export outputs cannot change (AL-A8).

Design:

- **Production stops.** `FillLadder::fast_rung_until` with the stops S1 (AL-FR0, the S1 prefix and the copy into the rung), FR1 to FR5, FR8 and AL-FR9, on the export's request (e = −3, nonzero). Each stop is timed on its own over the cell's ten paths, and a stage's cost is the difference of adjacent medians, as in the spike's SL-D1 to SL-D4. One untimed pass per stop records the `fill_filter` decided/fallback counts of F0.2, F1 and F0.3; their stop-to-stop difference is each stage's share.
- **The mirror, for the nested stops.** `fill_ladder.rs` keeps its stage internals private, and this unit's file scope ([section 6.1](p3-alt-a1-fill-ladder-contract.md#61-units): `fill_ladder_tests.rs` or a `cfg(test)` diagnostic module) excludes it. That scope is the constraint; `cfg(test)` stops would not change the release WASM. The new module therefore holds a statement-for-statement copy of FR1 to FR5 on the same S1 view. It makes the same `Interval`, `orient2d` and `segment_relation` calls in the same order, without the `cfg(test)` `fault()` reads, which are constant `false` outside test builds. Its sub-stages are timed as isolated kernels over state prepared once per path, untimed.
- **Mirror fidelity, checked before any timing.** On every one of the 1,270 B0 corpus paths, the mirror's FR1-FR5 result equals the production `fast_rung_until(.., Stage::Fr5)` result. On a pass, its observations also equal the production `FastStats`: the bits of the largest |d_m|² bound, the sweep candidates, and the clearance step 2 and step 3 counts. Result: 1,121 paths pass FR1-FR5 in production, and 0 differ.
- **Kernel cross-checks, untimed, once per prepared state.** The isolated kernels must reproduce what the counted stage pass observed: the `fr2_terms` result has the bits of the largest |d_m|² bound, `fr5_scan` returns the candidate count, `fr5_box_gap` the box-gap decisions, and `fr5_relation` the number of step-3 pairs. All hold on every timed path. The kernels share the prepared state in slot order: slot 0 (FR1) zeroes `controls` and `deviation_squared`, and slot 2 (FR2) restores them before the later slots read them; a code comment records this dependency.
- **Test-build hook cost.** In test builds `fill_filter::Interval`'s R1 widening reads a thread-local fault switch on every operation. A calibration times a dependent chain of `add` and `mul` against a copy without that read: 14.11 against 13.61 ns per pair (ratio 1.04). So the test-build hooks barely distort these timings.
- **Cells.** The timing runs on the spike's diagnostic cells, P2, P4 and P8 (26 cells, the first ten paths of each), as the [spike record's diagnostic split](../benchmarks/results/2026-10-18_p3_p3-b0-spike-ladder-v1_native_desktop-dkvusav_20261018T213619Z-89016cc9.md#diagnostic-stage-split) did. Every timed path passes FR1-FR5.

Command (release, pinned Rust 1.94.1 through `.tools/rust`, one test thread; defaults: 3 warm-up and 21 timed rounds, each running every kernel 50 times):

The corpus path must be absolute, because cargo runs the test binary in `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel`; from the repository root:

```text
P3_B0R_B0_CORPUS=$PWD/artifacts/p3-b0r-identity/20261019T182542Z-646e543c/b0-corpus.bin \
  rustup run 1.94.1 cargo test --manifest-path packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/Cargo.toml \
  --release --lib --locked --offline fill_ladder_diag_tests -- --ignored --nocapture --test-threads 1
```

The corpus file is the AL-U3 re-baseline run's B0 corpus, SHA-256 `206cf6e9…9796`, the frozen B0 corpus. The run passed in 98 s. Its full output, with the per-cell rows of every table, belongs in the PR.

**Sub-stage split.** Mirror kernels; medians in µs per pass over a cell's ten paths, summed over the 26 cells; diagnostics, not evidence. Kernels are isolated, so a stage's sub-kernels need not add up to its own kernel. Per-cell kernel medians are noisy and can even exceed their stage's figure: at P8/s=8, `restrict` plus terms is 605 against FR2's 465. The sums over 26 cells are steadier.

| Stage and sub-stage                            | Sum, 26 cells | Share of mirror FR1-FR5 | P2/s=16 | P4/s=16 | P8/s=8 | Work, 26 cells                               |
| ---------------------------------------------- | ------------: | ----------------------: | ------: | ------: | -----: | -------------------------------------------- |
| FR1 structure                                  |        193.20 |                    2.2% |    6.65 |   20.15 |  11.63 | 7,740 edges, all cubic leaves                |
| — source lookups (`source_end`, `resolve`)     |         76.62 |                    0.9% |    2.97 |    8.73 |   4.80 | 8,000 lookups, 38,940 sources scanned        |
| FR2 deviation                                  |      5,941.43 |                   67.2% |  186.12 |  506.52 | 465.14 | —                                            |
| — interval de Casteljau (`restrict`)           |      3,679.93 |                   41.6% |   76.28 |  300.73 | 456.71 | 16,420 `split_half` (depth 1 P2, 2 P4, 3 P8) |
| — \|d_m\|² terms and comparisons with δ²       |      1,638.14 |                   18.5% |   56.63 |  134.35 | 148.34 | 30,960 terms                                 |
| — residual (FR2 − `restrict` − terms)          |        623.36 |                    7.1% |       — |       — |      — | stores and whole-function codegen            |
| FR3 closure and AL-M5                          |          1.55 |                    0.0% |    0.04 |    0.07 |   0.06 | 0 contracted contours                        |
| FR4 monotone                                   |      2,196.12 |                   24.8% |   65.33 |  188.59 | 198.45 | 7,740 pairs, 61,920 interval dot signs       |
| — chords                                       |        553.06 |                    6.3% |   16.94 |   51.86 |  42.50 | 15,480 dot signs                             |
| — control steps                                |      1,625.25 |                   18.4% |   52.21 |  125.61 | 132.75 | 46,440 dot signs                             |
| FR5 clearance                                  |        507.25 |                    5.7% |   13.36 |   49.19 |  56.81 | —                                            |
| — sort keys and merge sort                     |        151.87 |                    1.7% |    4.60 |   13.60 |  12.03 | 7,740 edges                                  |
| — sweep scan and consecutive skip              |         84.17 |                    1.0% |    3.67 |    7.91 |   7.42 | 17,008 visits, 7,740 skips, 9,268 candidates |
| — step 1, box gap                              |        255.95 |                    2.9% |    9.19 |   20.28 |  28.88 | 9,268 tests, all decided                     |
| — step 2, `segment_relation`                   |          0.17 |                    0.0% |    0.01 |    0.01 |   0.01 | 0 pairs                                      |
| — step 3, point tests                          |          0.78 |                    0.0% |    0.04 |    0.03 |   0.03 | 0 pairs                                      |
| Mirror FR1-FR5 (sum of the five stage kernels) |      8,839.55 |                    100% |  271.50 |  764.52 | 732.09 | —                                            |

**Production stops, same run.** Differences of medians, summed over the 26 cells, in µs: S1 1,479.66; FR1 263.48; FR2 6,129.84; FR3 −199.42; FR4 2,730.90; FR5 833.04; FR6-FR8 1,104.67; AL-FR9 665.93. The fast rung totals 13,008.09, and FR1-FR5 9,757.84, or 75% of it (the spike record: 76-82% at the largest cells). Per cell the differences are noisy, and a small stage can come out negative, as in the spike record. That is why the sub-stages are measured as isolated mirror kernels. The mirror's FR1-FR5 sum is 91% of the production stops' FR1-FR5 sum.

**Filters.** FR1 to FR5 record no F0.2, F1 or F0.3 call on the 26 cells: the certificate's cost is interval arithmetic and control flow, and no orientation filter is involved. FR6 to FR8 record 27,963 F1 decisions (as in the spike record) and AL-FR9 7,220, all decided, with no fallback. F0.2 and F0.3 record nothing.

**Ranked hot spots and their candidate class** (section 5.1). Each is a candidate, not a decision; each AL-U4b PR names its class with its argument.

1. **FR2 interval de Casteljau, 41.6%.** 16,420 `split_half` calls, about 225-240 ns per call per pass, which is twelve interval add-and-multiply pairs. **AL-C2 with AL-C1**, prefix-shared subdivision. `split_half` computes all six midpoints whatever `right` is, so one call can yield both children by the same operations on the same inputs (AL-C1). Leaves of one cubic are consecutive in edge order, and here every leaf of a cell has the same depth d. If a cubic's leaves cover it, caching each level along the leaf order (AL-C2) needs one call per internal node, 2^d − 1 per cubic, instead of d·2^d. That saves about 50% at d = 1 (P2), 62.5% at d = 2 (P4) and 71% at d = 3 (P8). Hoisting `Interval::point(0.5)` out of `midpoint` is AL-C1, since it is a construction with no arithmetic.
2. **FR2 |d_m|² terms, 18.5%.** 30,960 terms. Each leaf also builds (2p_start + p_end)/3 and (p_start + 2p_end)/3, with two interval divisions. **AL-C1**: within a contour, `end.mul(two)` of edge i is `start.mul(two)` of edge i + 1, the same operation on the same operand. **Primary ruling, 2026-10-20: AL-C3 does not apply here.** `deviation_squared` and `FastStats::max_deviation_squared` stay bit-identical under AL-U4b: AL-M5's operand is FR2's enclosure ([contract section 3.3](p3-alt-a1-fill-ladder-contract.md#33-the-strengthened-micro-closure-condition-al-m5)), section 5.1 lets only logical counters change, and SL-O1 reads `maxDeviationSquared` from the dump. Only AL-C1 and AL-C2 apply to FR2.
3. **FR4 control steps, 18.4%.** 46,440 interval dot signs. Each edge's steps R_{m+1} − R_m are computed twice, once in each of its two pairs. **AL-C1**: compute the steps once per edge (the contract's own example). **AL-C3**: a dot-sign filter accepted when |fl(x)| exceeds a proved bound on the interval's half-width, which must include the enclosures' widths (the contract's own example).
4. **FR2 residual, 7.1%.** FR2's kernel minus `restrict` and the terms: 5,941.43 − 3,679.93 − 1,638.14 = 623 µs. It is the stores into the certified edge and the whole function's codegen, not copies. **AL-C4**: access each edge by reference, with `controls` stored in place.
5. **FR4 chords, 6.3%.** 15,480 dot signs of exact point differences; each chord is computed twice. **AL-C1** for the sharing. **AL-C3**: a binary64 sign filter with an a-priori error bound, since the inputs are exact.
6. **FR5 box gap, 2.9%.** 9,268 candidates, every one decided at step 1. **AL-C1**: per-edge box extremes once per edge (min and max only, no rounding). **AL-C4**: no copy of two `CertEdge` values per candidate.
7. **FR1, 2.2%,** of which the lookups are 0.9%: a linear scan of the decoded sources per edge. **AL-C4**.
8. **FR5 sort, 1.7%, and scan, 1.0%.** **AL-C4**: index types, the merge-sort buffers, and no copy of two `CertEdge` values per scan visit in `consecutive` (17,008 visits).
9. **FR3 and FR5 steps 2 and 3, 0%.** These cells have no contracted contour and no step 2 or 3 pair, so there is nothing to optimize here; the corpus's O1 paths do reach FR3.

FR2 and FR4 together are 92% of the mirror's certificate, and both are interval arithmetic. FR5, the sweep, is 5.7%. Outside the certificate, which section 5.1 does not cover, the S1 prefix is 11% of the fast rung and FR6 to AL-FR9 14%.

Deviations and notes:

- **Mirror instead of nested stops in `fill_ladder.rs`.** Section 5.1 describes "nested stops inside FR2 to FR5". This unit's files are `fill_ladder_tests.rs` or a `cfg(test)` module (section 6.1), so the stops cannot live in `fill_ladder.rs`; `cfg(test)` stops there would not have changed the release WASM, so the file scope is the only constraint. The mirror, with its fidelity check on all 1,270 B0 paths, is the substitute. An AL-U4b change to `fill_ladder.rs` does not update the mirror. The fidelity check fails if a change alters an outcome or an observation, but it cannot see a change in cost, so a re-measurement after an optimization must first bring the mirror up to date.
- **Counts that are new.** The work counts (lookups, splits, terms, dot signs, visits, skips, box decisions and the point-test branches) are the mirror's own logical counters. They are diagnostics and are not `FastStats` fields.
- **AL-A8 NOT RUN.** `pnpm test:p3-fill-identity` was not run: no non-test source changed and the WASM SHA-256 equals `main`'s, so no identity layer can differ.
- **AL-A5 and AL-I3.** The CI-layer AL-I3 runs inside `pnpm test:geometry`, which passes. The full-corpus AL-I3 is not re-run: `fill_ladder.rs` is unchanged since AL-U3, so its full-corpus result carries forward ([AL-U1](#alt-a1-al-u1-implementation), [AL-U3](#alt-a1-al-u3-implementation)).

### Alt-A1 AL-U4b1 shared subdivision

Unit AL-U4b1, the first certificate optimization under [section 5.1](p3-alt-a1-fill-ladder-contract.md#51-a-certificate-stage-optimization) of the Alt-A1 contract, light tier with one independent diff review. It applies hot spots 1 and 2 of the [AL-U4a split](#alt-a1-al-u4a-diagnostic-split) to FR2, with only classes AL-C1 and AL-C2. Under the Primary ruling recorded there, `deviation_squared` and `FastStats::max_deviation_squared` stay bit-identical, and AL-C3 is not used. AL-U4a was merged through PR #157 (`4f48284`). Acceptance check: AL-A11 (`pnpm test:p3-fill-identity` plain mode PASS against `4f48284`, AL-A5, AL-A7, the seeded defects still detected), with AL-A2, AL-A8, AL-A12 and AL-A16. The timings below are diagnostics, **not evidence**. This entry makes no performance claim, and precision stays UNVERIFIED.

Files:

- `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/fill_ladder.rs`: FR2's `deviation`, the new `Subdivision` with `split_both`, `midpoint` and `same_bits`, and a `cfg(test)` hook `shared_restrict_bits`. The spike's `restrict` and `split_half` are removed from the module. No check, threshold or reason changes.
- `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/fill_ladder_tests.rs`, by additions only: the equivalence test and its `cfg(test)` copy of the AL-U3 `restrict`, `split_half` and `midpoint`; after the review, the `max_deviation_squared` bit comparisons against the spike (review item S1 below).
- `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/fill_ladder_diag_tests.rs`: the AL-U4a mirror, brought up to date (see the deviations below).

Design and bit-identity argument (the code comments carry each class's argument):

- **(a) Shared subdivision, AL-C2 with AL-C1.** `split_half` computed all six midpoints whatever `right` was. `split_both` makes the same six `midpoint` calls on the same operands, in the same order, and returns both halves; each half has the bits `split_half` gave it (AL-C1). `Subdivision` is bounded stack scratch, new in each `deviation` call. It holds, for the last source seen, the root and both halves of every node it has split along the last leaf's path, keyed by the prefix index (n − 1) >> (d − j) at level j (AL-C2). The source is compared by bits, so `IPoint::exact` gives the same root. The key at level j equals the leaf's only when the first j bits of the cell agree. A cached level-j node is therefore the result of the same j steps on the same inputs as the uncached path, and every leaf's R0-R3 are bit-identical to the spike's `restrict`. When a cubic's leaves are consecutive and cover it, each internal node is split once: 2^d − 1 calls instead of d·2^d. A leaf deeper than `MAX_LEAF_DEPTH`, which FR1 already rejects, takes the uncached loop.
- **(b) `Interval::point(0.5)` hoisted, AL-C1.** It is built once per `split_both` and passed to `midpoint`. It is a construction with no arithmetic, so the operand of every `mul` is unchanged.
- **(c) 2 p_end shared with the next 2 p_start, AL-C1.** `deviation` keeps the previous leaf's p_end and its `end.mul(two)`. The next leaf's `start.mul(two)` is that value only when its p_start has the same bits (`same_bits`, so signed zeros stay distinct). Both are `IPoint::exact(p).mul(two)` on bit-identical p; otherwise the product is computed as before.
- **No reordering.** Every check runs in the same order with the same operands: the four (or, under defect (2), two) |d_m|² terms against δ², then the stores. Only the order in which some pure interval products are computed changes. `fill_filter::Interval` reads only the test-build fault switch, which no call changes.

Equivalence test `fill_ladder_tests::al_u4b1_shared_subdivision_is_bit_identical_to_the_per_leaf_restrict`:

- It compares the shared path against the AL-U3 copy, bit for bit, on 85,690 leaves. They include covering leaf orders at depths 0-8, a 20,000-step random walk over 25 cubics, and depth-20 runs. The walk mixes depths, sibling runs, coarser ancestors, revisits, and a source copy that differs only in the sign of a zero.
- Coordinates come from several families: small integers, dyadic fractions, wide exponents, subnormals, signed zeros, and values near `f64::MAX`. 9,993 leaves have undecided (non-finite) controls, and the test requires both kinds.
- Result: 0 differ. Each covering sequence makes exactly 2^d − 1 splits.
- Two mutations were each detected: comparing sources by value instead of by bits gave 7 differences, and an off-by-one in the key gave 20,000.


### Alt-A1 AL-U4b2 FR4 shared dots

Unit AL-U4b2, the second certificate optimization under [section 5.1](p3-alt-a1-fill-ladder-contract.md#51-a-certificate-stage-optimization) of the Alt-A1 contract, light tier with one independent diff review. It applies the AL-C1 parts of hot spots 3 and 5 of the [AL-U4a split](#alt-a1-al-u4a-diagnostic-split) to FR4 (SL-C2), the monotone check. It uses only class AL-C1; no AL-C3 filter is added (see the deviations below). AL-U4b1 was merged through PR #158 (`85f7ef9`). Acceptance check: AL-A11 (`pnpm test:p3-fill-identity` plain mode PASS against `85f7ef9`, AL-A5, AL-A7, the seeded defects still detected), with AL-A2, AL-A8, AL-A12 and AL-A16. The timings below are diagnostics, **not evidence**. This entry makes no performance claim, and precision stays UNVERIFIED.

Files:

- `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/fill_ladder.rs`: `FastRung::monotone` now calls the new `monotone_contour` once per contour, and the new `MonotoneEdge` holds one edge's operands. Two `cfg(test)` hooks are added: `FastRung::monotone_inputs` and `monotone_trace`, with the type `MonotoneTestEdge`. No check, threshold or reason changes.
- `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/fill_ladder_tests.rs`, by additions only: the equivalence tests and their `cfg(test)` copy of the AL-U4b1 FR4.
- `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/fill_ladder_diag_tests.rs`: the mirror's FR4 brought up to date, under the Primary ruling recorded in the [AL-U4b1 entry](#alt-a1-al-u4b1-shared-subdivision).

Design and decision-equality argument (the code comments carry it):

- **Per-edge operands once, AL-C1.** The spike's FR4 computed, for each consecutive pair (e, f), both chords `exact_difference(end, start)` and both edges' control steps `controls[m + 1].sub(controls[m])`. So each edge's operands were computed twice, once in the pair (previous, e) and once in (e, next). They depend only on the edge, not on the pair's w = p_end(f) − p_start(e). `MonotoneEdge::new` computes them once, by the same operations on the same operands, so each has the bits of both earlier computations. In each contour, edge 0's operands are computed first and kept for the cyclic pair (last, first); every other edge's are computed when it enters a pair as f and reused when it is e in the next pair.
- **Same dots, same decisions, same order.** Every decided quantity is still `dot(operand, w)` followed by `proved_positive`, on operands with the spike's bits. The order is unchanged: ⟨u_e, w⟩, then ⟨u_f, w⟩ only if the first passed, then e's steps and f's steps, with `STRUCTURE` for an unset piece at the spike's position. So the first failing reason and the outcome are the same for every input. Defect (3), chords only, still skips the steps. `MonotoneEdge::new` may compute f's steps before a chord check fails; `fill_filter::Interval` reads only the test-build fault switch, which nothing changes during FR4, so that work has no effect.
- **The `observe` callback.** `monotone_contour` passes each decided dot interval to a callback. Production passes a no-op closure, which inlines away; only `monotone_trace` records the intervals.
- **Logical counters.** None change: the mirror's pair and dot counts are the same, 7,740 pairs and 61,920 dots on the 26 cells.

Equivalence tests, both by additions in `fill_ladder_tests.rs`. Both FR4 versions record the bits [lo, hi] of every decided dot interval in order, and the result. Equal traces therefore mean the same dots on the same operands, the same decisions in the same order, and the same reason. Each case is compared with and without defect (3)'s chords-only switch.

- Debug test `al_u4b2_fr4_shared_operands_decide_as_before`. The real-path cases also compare the hook's result with the production stop after FR4, and the fixtures do so with and without the defect (3) fault. Results:
  - 21 fixtures reach FR4: 42 cases, 778 dots, 0 differ (38 pass, 4 `MONOTONE`).
  - 41 debug-sample paths reach FR4: 82 cases, 8,100 dots, 0 differ (78 pass, 4 `MONOTONE`).
  - Random edges: 20,000 cases of one to three contours with 1 to 10 edges each, so 40,000 compared runs and 368,078 dots, of which 14,016 are undecided. 0 differ: 7,997 pass, 30,353 `MONOTONE` and 1,650 `STRUCTURE`. The contours are convex polygons at scales 2^−80 to 2^79, or random points from the AL-U4b1 coordinate families. Their pieces are leaves or segments, with controls on the chords as exact or widened enclosures, occasionally undecided, with reversed steps and unset pieces.
- Ignored release test `al_u4b2_full_corpus_fr4_shared_operands_decide_as_before`, every corpus path that passes FR1-FR3 (B0 framed corpus and `p3-b0r-random/v1`):
  - B0: 2,362 cases, 385,160 dots, 0 differ (all pass).
  - `p3-b0r-random/v1`: 19,590 cases, 1,753,151 dots, 0 differ (17,745 pass, 1,845 `MONOTONE`).
- Two mutations were each detected: keeping edge 0's operands for every pair made 40 of the 42 fixture cases differ, and using e's steps twice made 8 differ.

**Diagnostic, not evidence.** The AL-U4a command, unchanged. For a same-session comparison, the same diagnostic file was also run against `85f7ef9`'s `fill_ladder.rs` (the kernel crate and its test fixtures exported with `git archive` to a scratch directory), immediately before the branch run. Both runs PASS, with mirror fidelity 1,270 B0 paths and 0 differ, and every kernel cross-check holds. The new `FR4.AL-U4b1` kernel, the AL-U4b1 FR4 kept in the mirror, passes on every timed path.

Medians, summed over the 26 cells, in µs:

| Quantity                                     | `85f7ef9` | This branch | Same-run kernel comparison (branch run)     |

| Production S1 (no change, a machine control) |  1,353.38 |    1,198.50 | —                                           |

Deviations and notes:

- **No AL-C3 filter.** Section 5.1 permits AL-C3 at FR4; its own example is an FR4 dot sign. It requires a per-site proof that the filter's "proved" implies the interval's "proved", with the interval test run unchanged on "undecided". Here the decision is `proved_positive` on the _computed_ interval: its lower endpoint after R1 widening at every operation, including the enclosures of the chord's exact differences and FR2's control enclosures, and with both endpoints finite. A binary64 filter would therefore need a bound on the gap between its own value and that lower endpoint, not on the true value, covering the one-ulp widenings, the subnormal range and the enclosure widths of the steps. For the steps, computing that bound reads both endpoints of every operand, which is most of the interval dot's own work. A filter that proves positive where the interval is undecided would admit more paths, which section 5.1 forbids. The expected gain did not justify that proof obligation in this unit, so it is left out; a later unit may propose one with its proof.
- **`fill_ladder_diag_tests.rs` is edited** under the AL-U4b1 Primary ruling. The mirror's FR4 follows `monotone_contour` statement for statement; its chords and steps sub-stage kernels compute only their own per-edge operands. The AL-U4b1 FR4 stays as the extra kernel `FR4.AL-U4b1`, with an untimed check that it passes on every timed path. The fidelity check is unchanged. The file is on the identity run's `--allow-test-change` list.
- **The equivalence test needs `cfg(test)` hooks in `fill_ladder.rs`.** `CertEdge` and `IPoint` stay private, so `monotone_inputs` exports the certified edges after FR3, and `monotone_trace` runs `monotone_contour` itself on them. They are functions, not an inline test module, so O5's inline-module check is unaffected.

### Alt-A1 AL-U5 AL-S1 harness

Unit AL-U5 of the [Alt-A1 production contract](p3-alt-a1-fill-ladder-contract.md#61-units): the AL-S1 harness, runner, analysis, validator, the ratio mode and the root script, following [section 5.3](p3-alt-a1-fill-ladder-contract.md#53-c-product-stage-verification-scenario-al-s1-and-the-verdict), with the ratio mode of [section 5.2](p3-alt-a1-fill-ladder-contract.md#52-b-b0-w-the-wasmnative-ratio). Acceptance for this unit: AL-A13 (the AL-S1 part), with AL-A2, AL-A12 and AL-A16. The functional run below is **not evidence**; only AL-U7's reference record carries performance evidence, and precision stays UNVERIFIED.

Files:

- New test-only harness `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/p3_fill_ladder_bench.rs`, declared by one `cfg(test)` line appended at the end of `lib.rs`, so the release WASM stays byte-identical to `main`'s. It reuses `p3_b0_bench.rs`'s corpus reader, clock-quantum probe, shuffle, FNV and JSON helpers and AL-U2's `fill_ladder_dump_tests::{read_input, dump}`, all unchanged.
- New `tooling/run-p3-fill-ladder-benchmark.mjs` and `.d.mts`, new `tests/geometry/p3-fill-ladder/analysis.ts`, new `tests/geometry/p3-fill-ladder-analysis.test.ts`.
- `package.json`: the root script `benchmark:p3-fill-ladder` only. `.gitignore`: `artifacts/p3-fill-ladder/` (see the deviations).

Design:

- **Timing, E1 and E3.** Three ignored release tests, run only by the runner. `run_ladder_decision` is one repetition with B0's sampling. Per admitted cell it constructs one `FillEngine`, reserves it outside timing, and sizes its output in an untimed preparation call on every input. A timed sample is one `FillEngine::process` call on the cell's framed 10-path `fill_v0` input (nonzero, e = −3, request id = path index), then each path alone in its own one-path call, timed individually for the E3 proxy. Each window ends after the FNV-1a fold of that call's output bytes `[0, result_len)`. Inputs are copied into the engine before each window. An allocation-counted untimed sample follows, and the engine's storage (addresses and capacities) is compared before and after the samples. Linearity on P4/s=16 is B0's: 20 ten-path batches, then 2 warm-up and 5 timed 100-path batches.
- **Admission and ladder mix.** Per path, one one-path `process` call with the allocation counter around the call only. Its status is the V2 path status name (`OK`, `WORK_LIMIT`, …) or `BATCH_STATUS(n)`, with V, T and the boundary pairs. Rung, reason, exact step and topology selection come through `build_path` (`FillEngine::build_one`), untimed, and the S1 flat command count from a separate `prepare_prefix` probe. B0's cap codes all reach the export as `WORK_LIMIT` (V2 section e), which is the configuration's cap list. Check 5b compares the ladder mix across repetitions; check 5 the batch checksums and the chained one-path checksums.
- **E5, labelled.** Each repetition records `size_of::<FillEngine>()` and the admission engine's `heap_bytes()`. `heap_bytes()` is the workspace, scratch and ladder storage that `fill_v0_reserve` allocates from `FILL_V0_LIMITS`; it does not depend on the input and excludes the input and output buffers. Each timed cell also records its prepared engine's `heapBytes` and `bufferBytes` (input plus output capacity after the preparation sample). The analysis reports `inlineBytes`, `admissionHeapBytes`, the set of `cellHeapBytes` (one value expected) and `maxBufferBytes`.
- **Oracle run, `run_ladder_oracle`.** One untimed process after the repetitions. The runner writes the `P3FLDIN1` input of the full dump set of section 4.2: all 1,270 B0 corpus paths and the 2,000 paths of `p3-fill-ladder-random/v1`, 3,270 entries, with the family's SHA-256 `a597510d…7bbc` checked. The test writes AL-U2's `p3-fill-ladder-dump/1` dump. Per path it records the ladder outcome through `build_path`, and checks AL-I2 on every path whose rung is not fast: the one-path export output must equal the `exact_only` output byte for byte. Over the B0 corpus it records the exact rung as B0 called it (`attempt`, or `attempt_transverse` on X) and B0's batch checksums. `evaluateLadderOracle` then applies these gates:
  - SL-O1 to SL-O3 with AL-U2's `evaluateEntry` on every entry;
  - SL-O4 against the 2026-10-17 record, whose configuration hash is recomputed and whose analysis B0's `validateRecord` re-checks first, together with every exact outcome, the 35 batch checksums and the dump's completeness (each expected name exactly once);
  - AL-I2 at module level (`ladderExactAgrees`) and at export level;
  - harness: zero allocations in every ladder call, the `build_path` outcome equal to the module ladder's, and kernel revision 2;
  - the `MESH_CHECK` count.

  AL-I3, SL-O5, SL-O6 and O1/O2 are recorded as observations.

- **Verdict.** B0's fits, checks 1-8, estimates and budgets come from B0's exported functions, imported unchanged, as the spike's analysis did. The verdict is labelled `provisional at k = 2`. The run is `INVALID` for any of: a failed oracle gate (a gate with nothing checked does not pass), a nonzero `MESH_CHECK` count, a checksum mismatch, storage moved during timing, or a kernel revision other than 2. The functional profile's verdict is `NONE`. The runner writes `raw-record.json` first, then the validated `record.json` and `summary.md` under `artifacts/p3-fill-ladder/<run-id>/`. It exits nonzero, after writing them, when the run is invalid. Provenance follows the spike runner: `--environment-json`, a `tasklist` process listing kept as an artifact, and no CPU-load sampling. Committed records will be named `YYYY-MM-DD_p3_p3-fill-ladder-v1_native_desktop-dkvusav_<run-id>.md` and `.json`.
- **How the record holds k.** The configuration's `decision` is B0's, with k = 2, so AL-S1's own verdict is provisional. `analysis.estimates` also keeps the k-free t̂ (`perPathNs`), t̂_p95 (`p95Ns`) and M̂, plus the reported payload with boundary pairs. AL-S2 supplies k̂ and k̂₁. `productEstimates` and `productVerdict(analysis, kHat, s2, kHatOnePath)` compute T̂ = 1,000 · t̂ · k̂ and t̂_edit = t̂_p95 · k̂, with t̂_p95 · k̂₁ as context only, under B0's rule. AL-U6 owns the combined record and its validator.
- **Product verdict signals (Primary decision after the AL-U5 review).** `productVerdict` returns:
  - `NONE` when the AL-S1 analysis's verdict is `NONE`, that is, for any non-reference profile, as B0's rule gives;
  - `INVALID` when AL-S1 has an invalid reason, or when AL-S2 passes `s2.invalid = true`;
  - `INCONCLUSIVE` when an AL-S1 check fails, or when AL-S2 passes `s2.valid = false`;
  - otherwise FEASIBLE or INFEASIBLE on the three budgets.

  AL-U6 must pass `s2.invalid = true` for a section 5.3 no-verdict cause: a browser checksum that differs from the native one. It must pass `s2.valid = false` for any other failed section 5.2 validity item: an incomplete repetition, an UNVERIFIED primary largest cell, a reserve, growth or retry during timing, or incomplete metadata.

- **Ratio mode.** `run_ladder_ratio`, exported to AL-S2's runner as `runRatioMode({ profile, output })`, is outside AL-S1's record, hash and verdict. `ratioModeConfig(profile)` defines it, and AL-S2's configuration is to embed it:
  - per repetition, a fresh process; the 34 decision cells, then the one-path cells (path 0 of P2/s=16, P4/s=16 and P8/s=8), each shuffled as in B0;
  - per sample, R = 100 (one-path: 1,000) consecutive `process` calls, each followed by the decoded fold, the window ending after the last fold;
  - the decoded fold: FNV-1a chained over the calls; per path, the status u32 LE; on `OK` also V, T and the pair count u32 LE, then the block's position f64 bits, indices and pairs, all LE. AL-U6's browser fold must reproduce it from the adapter's results.

  `analyzeRatioNative` gives each per-path median and UNVERIFIED flag, and the native validity items: complete repetitions and cells, revision, no allocation or storage change, one checksum per cell, and no UNVERIFIED largest cell.

- **Validator.** `validateLadderRecord` checks the schema, the scenario identity, the configuration hash, the configuration pinned to `scenarioConfig(profile)` (as SL-U3's review required), the corpus hash, and the stored analysis against a recomputation.

**Configuration hashes.** `configHash(scenarioConfig('reference'))` of AL-S1 is `8f07f1467b1e396fd1a9c02e6f27c17131cf203e287b5ea690391536b7f3672e`, asserted in the analysis test. The functional profile's is `2d895a3d732a7b2f0db5e215a8ab4880e92eb1c5f29cf7505bcd960ad2939246`. B0's (`a10c98e9…9271`) and the spike's (`0daa96c0…2267`) are unchanged and asserted. `ratioModeConfig('reference')` hashes to `ff36ec78a920d704df431cf411f9c064bf1d3a0cb21523c83857a6bdad9ef9c3`, also asserted in the analysis test, for AL-U6 to embed.

**Notes for AL-U6.** AL-S2's source manifest must include `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/p3_fill_ladder_bench.rs` and `tooling/run-p3-fill-ladder-benchmark.mjs`. The first is the native leg's harness; the kernel `src` root covers it. AL-S2 must pass `s2.invalid` and `s2.valid` to `productVerdict` as stated above. The browser fold must reproduce `ratioModeConfig().fold`.

Deviations and notes:

- **`.gitignore`.** `artifacts/p3-fill-ladder/` is added, outside the unit's file list, as SL-U3 added the spike's directory: raw run output must stay out of `pnpm check`'s Prettier check and out of the reference profile's clean-worktree rule. The committed record's `.prettierignore` entry is left to AL-U7.
- **AL-I3 is an observation here.** Section 5.3 names SL-O1 to SL-O4, AL-I2 and `MESH_CHECK` as the oracle run's gates. AL-I3 is evaluated on every entry and reported (identical, allowed `RANGE` and AL-M5 differences, other differences), but it does not invalidate the run. The reviewer should confirm this reading.
- **Storage and revision invalidate.** A moved or regrown engine storage during timing, the native analogue of AL-S2's "no reserve or growth", and a kernel revision other than 2 are added to the invalid reasons. Allocation counts in timed and pre-check samples are reported, as in B0 and the spike. Allocation in a ladder call in the oracle run fails the harness gate.
- **E3 sampling.** B0 timed each path inside its batch. With the export a batch is one call, so each timed sample is the batch call followed by the ten one-path calls, each timed on its own. This gives the same 10 E3 times per sample.
- **O1/O2 through `build_path`.** O1 and O2 come from the oracle run's `build_path` records, with the module ladder's rung cross-checked against them. On X paths the exact rung is V2's cascade, whose triangle-free retry publishes; the expectation is that every X path falls back with `CROSSING`.
- **AL-S1 cap list.** The configuration's cap list is `['WORK_LIMIT']`: B0's cap codes are kernel error strings, and the export maps every one of them to the path status `WORK_LIMIT` (V2 section e). `CombinedByteLimit` and `EmissionStatus` map to a batch internal error instead.
- **Worktree toolchain.** The implementation worktree had no `.tools`. Its `.tools/rust` is a junction to the main checkout's pinned toolchain, and every target directory stays inside the worktree. `.tools` is ignored, so nothing of this is committed.

### Alt-A1 AL-U6 AL-S2 WASM ratio

Unit AL-U6 of the [Alt-A1 production contract](p3-alt-a1-fill-ladder-contract.md#61-units): the AL-S2 page, runner, analysis, validator, the combined verdict and the root script, following [section 5.2](p3-alt-a1-fill-ladder-contract.md#52-b-b0-w-the-wasmnative-ratio) (B0-W) and the product verdict of [section 5.3](p3-alt-a1-fill-ladder-contract.md#53-c-product-stage-verification-scenario-al-s1-and-the-verdict). Light tier with one independent diff review. Acceptance for this unit: AL-A13 (the AL-S2 part), with AL-A2, AL-A12 and AL-A16. The functional run below is **not evidence**; only AL-U7's reference record carries performance evidence, and precision stays UNVERIFIED.

**Depends on AL-U5.** The runner imports `runRatioMode` from `tooling/run-p3-fill-ladder-benchmark.mjs`, and the analysis imports `ratioModeConfig`, `analyzeRatioNative`, `productVerdict` and `validateLadderRecord` from `tests/geometry/p3-fill-ladder/analysis.ts`, unchanged. AL-U5 merged first, through PR #161 (`e0130fa`).

Files:

- `tests/geometry-benchmark/p3-b0-wasm/` (the page, placed as the P2.5 page is): `index.html`, `main.ts`, `workload.ts`, `vite.config.ts` and `benchmark.spec.ts`; and the root `playwright.p3-b0-wasm.config.ts`, beside `playwright.p2-benchmark.config.ts`.
- `tooling/run-p3-b0-wasm-benchmark.mjs` and `.d.mts`: `pnpm benchmark:p3-b0-wasm --profile functional|reference [--environment-json <observations.json>] [--al-s1 <path>]`, with B0's argument parser and environment reader imported unchanged; `--al-s1` is parsed before them.
- `tests/geometry/p3-b0-wasm/analysis.ts` and `tests/geometry/p3-b0-wasm-analysis.test.ts`.
- `package.json` (the root script only), `.gitignore` (`artifacts/p3-b0-wasm/`) and `.prettierignore` (the future AL-S2 records, kept byte for byte as the B0 and spike records are).

Design:

- **One runner session.** The runner builds the release WASM with the cargo command `pnpm test:geometry` uses and records its SHA-256. It runs the native leg first: AL-U5's `runRatioMode`, the AL-S1 harness's ratio mode, unchanged. It then builds the Vite production page, with the WASM and the B0 framed corpus emitted as assets, and runs Playwright: Chrome, then Edge, headed in both profiles, one worker, a fresh page per repetition, no tracing, video or DevTools. Raw output goes to an exclusive `artifacts/p3-b0-wasm/<run-id>/`.
- **The page.** It verifies the SHA-256 of the WASM and the corpus. Outside timing, it decodes the corpus into the 34 decision cells' paths 0-9 as port requests: request id = path index, nonzero, e = −3. It drives `createFillMeshPort(module)`, one adapter per page. Each cell gets an untimed preparation sample, then warm-up and timed samples of R = 100 `buildFillMeshes` calls. The one-path cells get R = 1,000 on path 0. The order is B0's per-repetition shuffle, decision cells first, then the one-path cells, as the ratio mode orders them. The window runs from before the first call to after the last call's fold.
- **Checksum.** `Fnv1a64.result` reproduces `ratioModeConfig().fold` and the Rust `fold_decoded`: FNV-1a 64 over the status, V, T and the pair count, then the position, index and pair bytes, little-endian, chained over the calls. It is computed on two u32 words without BigInt. Its per-mesh `Uint8Array` views are created inside the window and counted in the browser time. The test checks it against a BigInt reference. The functional run checks it against the native leg on every cell.
- **No reserve, growth or retry while timing.** The page keeps a read-only handle on the instance the factory creates, by wrapping `WebAssembly.instantiate` only while `createFillMeshPort` runs; the adapter code is the package's, unchanged. Each warm-up and timed sample must advance `fill_v0_memory_epoch` by exactly R, which means one `process` per call and no reserve or OUTPUT_CAPACITY retry. It must also leave both arena capacities and the linear memory size unchanged.
- **Clock.** The P2.5 probe: the minimum positive `performance.now` increment. The preview serves the page cross-origin isolated (COOP/COEP), and the page records `crossOriginIsolated`. A sample shorter than 100 quanta makes its cell UNVERIFIED.
- **Ratio.** Per cell and repetition index, r = browser per-path median / native per-path median, where per-path = nearest-rank median sample / (R · paths). k_cell is the nearest-rank median of r over the repetitions. **k̂** is the maximum over Chrome and Edge of k_cell at P2/s=16, P4/s=16 and P8/s=8, labelled "browser port time over native export time in the test-harness build". **k̂₁** is the same at the one-path cells; it is report-only.
- **Validity, all required.**
  - `repetitions-complete`: every native and browser repetition, and every cell's browser and native checksum present. A missing browser, native leg or cell is reported here.
  - `checksums-equal-native`: only a present browser checksum that differs from a present native checksum fails it. This is the one item that gives no verdict (`s2.invalid`).
  - `largest-primary-verified`: browser and native.
  - `no-reserve-or-growth`: the browser epoch and storage check, and native zero allocations with stable storage.
  - `largest-cells-admitted`: every path `ok`.
  - `operation-identity`: page options, WASM and corpus SHA-256, `operationId` `'geometry-wasm/fill-v0+r2'`, port version 0. It also requires the page's cell order and one-path order, and the native leg's cell order, to equal B0's shuffle with seed `0x9e3779b9 + r`.
  - `native-leg-valid`: `analyzeRatioNative`.
  - `cross-origin-isolated`: every captured page reports `crossOriginIsolated`, as the configuration pins.
  - `al-s1-same-kernel`: the linked AL-S1 record's `run.sourceManifest` entries under `packages/geometry-wasm/kernel/src/`, plus the kernel `Cargo.toml` and `Cargo.lock`, must equal this run's, compared by the SHA-256 of their canonical JSON (`kernelSourceSha256`). The comparison is recorded as `analysis.alS1Kernel`. A difference makes the run INCONCLUSIVE, so an AL-S1 t̂ is never multiplied by a k̂ of another kernel.
  - `metadata-complete`: the README browser fields of every browser repetition record (channel, version, launch flags, viewport and device-pixel ratio). For the reference profile only, also the power and background observations and the AL-S1 link.
  - Interpretation: a page that is not visible for the whole repetition fails that repetition.
- **Combined verdict.** `combinedVerdict` passes two signals separately. `s2Invalid` is a checksum mismatch, which yields INVALID with no verdict. `s2Valid` covers every other validity item; if it is false, the verdict is INCONCLUSIVE. With both clear, AL-U5's `productVerdict(AL-S1 analysis, k̂, …, k̂₁)` applies B0's rule: T̂ = 1,000 · t̂ · k̂, t̂_edit = t̂_p95 · k̂, and M̂ from AL-S1, with t̂_p95 · k̂₁ as context only. A functional run has no verdict (NONE). A reference run without a usable AL-S1 link is INCONCLUSIVE.
- **AL-S1 link.** The candidates are the tracked, unmodified `docs/benchmarks/results/*_p3_p3-fill-ladder-v1_*.json` records whose profile is reference.
  - `--al-s1 <path>` names one candidate explicitly. A kernel difference is then recorded by `al-s1-same-kernel`, not refused.
  - Without it, the runner links the only candidate whose kernel sources equal this run's. If several match, it requires `--al-s1`, so a second AL-S1 record never blocks the runner. If none matches, there is no link, and the reference profile refuses to start.
  - The record's `alS1` holds its path, the SHA-256 of its bytes, its run ID, scenario, profile, configuration hash and kernel-source hash, and a copy of the analysis parts the verdict reads: estimates, checks, invalid reasons and verdict. The validator rebuilds this link from the linked bytes.
- **Validator `validateWasmRecord(record, alS1Bytes)`.** It checks the schema and scenario identity, the configuration hash, and that the configuration is pinned, equal to `scenarioConfig(profile)`. It checks the corpus SHA-256. It checks the native configuration against the embedded `ratioModeConfig` and its hash. For the link, it checks the SHA-256 of the linked bytes, re-validates them with `validateLadderRecord`, and rebuilds and compares the link. Finally, it recomputes the analysis.
- **Configuration.** It embeds `ratioModeConfig(profile)`, whose reference hash is `ff36ec78…f9c3`. The AL-S2 reference configuration hash is `f337d0efb6c457118c624dacc9bcd6a9946f2c495437282c987f20be884c2bfd`, pinned in the test; a change to AL-U5's ratio-mode configuration changes it.

Review follow-up. The independent diff review of PR #162 returned NOT READY with one blocking item. It and the should-fix items are applied on the same branch, as described in the design above:

- **B1.** A missing native leg, a whole missing browser, or a missing cell was classified as a checksum mismatch, and so as INVALID with a false reason. Now only two present checksums that differ are a mismatch. A missing checksum or leg fails `repetitions-complete` instead, which gives INCONCLUSIVE. Tests cover a whole missing browser, a missing native leg and a missing cell.
- **S1.** The `al-s1-same-kernel` item and `analysis.alS1Kernel` were added, and auto-discovery links only an AL-S1 record of this kernel. The test covers a changed kernel file and a manifest without kernel entries.
- **S2.** `--al-s1 <path>` was added, and the link rule is documented above.
- **S3.** `cross-origin-isolated` was added as a validity item.
- **S4.** The B0 shuffle orders are checked under `operation-identity`.
- **S5.** Neither the plan's Next line nor the B0 row is edited.

### V4 playground path page

Light tier, D8 checkpoint V4 ([D8](p3-d8-parallel-tracks.md)): a playground path page with fills, holes, both fill rules, pan, zoom and rotation, and headed screenshots. The user resumed feature development on 2026-10-20 (AL-U8 decision) and decided that on 2026-10-20 only, verification is skipped in favour of feature work: the independent diff review and long identity or benchmark runs are **NOT RUN — user exception 2026-10-20**. Functional evidence only: precision is **UNVERIFIED** against the 0.25 physical-pixel target until B4 has A04 evidence, and no Q4 coverage threshold is claimed. V4 closes no A01-A08 criterion and no C03-C05 item.

**Builder work cap (decision for the V3c gate).** The [V3c entry](#v3c-implementation) says V4 must not give a host a real port until B0's E3 result exists or a work cap for the builder is decided. V4 decides the cap, following the recommended direction:

- The cap is on the D8-interim TypeScript fringe builder (`buildPathFringe`), measured as the number of distinct vertex indices in the port mesh's `boundaryEdges`. It is `fringeBoundaryVertexCap: 128` in `PATH_MESH_LIMITS` (`path-mesh-pipeline.ts`); tests may lower it, production never does. The reviewer's timing on convex n-gons put n = 128 at 47 ms and n = 256 at 141 ms, so 128 bounds a single first build near the measured n = 128 cost; it is a functional bound, not a performance claim.
- A mesh above the cap is drawn **region-only**: its region triangles, no fringe (aliased edges, opacity applied once), through the existing region-only draw (`fringe: null`, the `c = 1` mode V3b's packer and shader already carry). The builder is never called for that cache entry.
- Each such cache entry queues one D8-interim notice, scope `renderer`, code `fringe-work-cap`, with the entry's tolerance exponent; the diagnostic message says the path "is drawn without its coverage fringe". It reuses `render.path-mesh-interim-failure` and its removal condition (V3 contract section h) and adds no public diagnostic code. Unlike every other section h code, this one does not hide the path; that is V3-Q2 option (c)'s behavior, scoped to the cap.
- The cap and the code are D8-interim. They are removed with the TypeScript builder when R2/K1 decides production placement, or when B0's E3 result replaces the bound.

Under the current kernel caps (24 source verbs, 16 segments, 4 contours, 72 flat commands, `cubic_fill.rs`) a port mesh rarely reaches 128 boundary vertices; the cap guards B1's raised caps.

Landed:

- `renderer-core`: the cap in `path-mesh-pipeline.ts` (limit, per-entry boundary-vertex count, region-only serving, one notice per entry, the message).
- `apps/playground`: `p3-v4-paths.html` with `src/p3-v4-paths.ts` (the host page) and `src/p3-v4-paths-scene.ts` (scene, views, probes). The page instantiates the real port with `createFillMeshPort` (geometry-wasm, `geometry-wasm/fill-v0+r2`) from the kernel WASM, passes it as `fillMesh` with `fillMeshMemoryBytes`, and offers drag to pan, wheel and buttons to zoom about a point, and Q/E or a slider to rotate the world container about the scene center. A side panel shows the view, port calls, cache bytes, kernel memory and diagnostics. The scene has eight paths in paint order: a cubic teardrop (nonzero); a square with a cubic circle of the same winding under evenodd (hole) and nonzero (no hole); the same square with a reversed circle under nonzero (hole); a pentagram under nonzero (center filled) and evenodd (center hole); a frame with two square holes (evenodd); and a cubic lens at opacity 0.6 over the frame. Shapes are sized so that every cubic path meshes within the kernel caps for `zoom · DPR ≤ 2` (probed against `fill-v0+r2`); beyond that the cubic paths report `work-limit` and are not drawn until B1.
- The playground manifest gains the `@vector-studio/geometry-wasm` workspace dependency and the project reference, the edge `tooling/boundary-policy.mjs` already allowed (P-B1). `vite.config.mjs` gains the page entry and a dev-server route `/__vector-studio/geometry-kernel.wasm` that serves the local kernel build (default `.tools/geometry-build/.../vector_studio_geometry_kernel.wasm`, override `VECTOR_STUDIO_GEOMETRY_WASM`). No WASM binary is committed; the static build has no kernel and the page then reports how to build it.
- V3-A18: as the criterion foresaw, V4 removes both V3-A18 unit tests and replaces them with V4 tests in the same files (`v3a-path-pipeline.test.ts`, `v3b-mesh-backend.test.ts`): only `src/p3-v4-paths.ts` (with the manifest and `tsconfig.json` edges) references `geometry-wasm` or injects `fillMesh`; the V3 smoke fixture still imports no `geometry-wasm`; every other playground file still has no `fillMesh`, `geometry-wasm` or mesh-pipeline reference.
- Tests: `tests/unit/v4-fringe-cap.test.ts` (6 tests: the production value 128; above the cap region-only with no builder call and one notice across frames and fringe buckets; at the cap the builder runs; one notice per new tolerance entry; with the real builder a 128-gon gets a fringe and a 129-gon draws region-only; the message). `tests/gpu/p3-v4-paths.spec.ts`: headed screenshots in Chrome and Edge of six gated views and one observed view. `.gitignore` gains `artifacts/p3-v4-paths/`.

- **WASM provenance on the dev server.** The page loads whatever kernel the dev server's path holds; the page and `observations.json` report its SHA-256, and the adapter rejects a kernel whose fill-v0 version or revision differs.
